ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u13	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present to you a government bill amending the excise tax law. The key change envisaged in the project is the indexing of excise duties on tobacco products and their substitutes and on alcoholic beverages. The purpose of this law is purely health-promoting. According to OECD and WHO studies... [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] I'm asking for peace in the courtroom. Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Finance Jan Sarnowski: ...to increase excise duty on products... I ask the Minister to take a moment. I ask all those who are not interested in this point to leave the room because we want to proceed calmly. I'll give you one and a half minutes. [[Wait a minute.]] I would very much like to ask those countries who are not interested in the point of making it possible to hold a debate. You're welcome, Minister. We're trying. Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Finance Jan Sarnowski: Marshal, thank you very much. According to OECD and World Health Organisation surveys, the increase in excise duty on tobacco and alcohol products leads to a reduction in their price availability. According to international organisations, this reduces the popularity of their consumption and thus reduces the negative effects of their consumption, especially among young people. The Ministry of Health and the medical environments consulted agree with this. Ladies and gentlemen! It is clear that the availability of drugs goes hand in hand with an increase in the wealth of society, but also with fashion. Plus, all over the world, there are more and more sophisticated products, designers, built-in campaigns, also paramedical ones, which brings more and more people, including young people, and often even children. Yet, according to the World Health Organization, tobacco and alcohol are the main risk factors for our health. Almost PLN 31 billion a year The National Agency for the Solution of Alcohol Problems sees as a cost for the Polish economy related to alcohol consumption in Poland. Since the increase in consumer prices is the most effective way to reduce their consumption, the global trend is to act as tax tools to reduce their price availability. In the same direction Poland wants to go. We want to introduce changes in a predictable, quantitative and, above all, gradual manner so that all market players can prepare for them. What's it gonna look like in detail? From 1 January next year we propose three changes: an increase in excise duty on alcoholic beverages by 10% Whereas excise duty on cider and perry with a capacity not exceeding 5% will not increase, which results directly from the government's strategy to support fruit production, which is one of the pillars of Polish agriculture; to increase the minimum rate of excise duty on cigarettes and to introduce a minimum rate of excise duty on smoking tobacco; to increase the rate of excise duty on novel products by increasing their quota rate. The amendment will not include excise duties on electronic cigarette liquids, as they are already taxed at European average level. These are the plans for 2022. What's gonna happen next? We propose the introduction of an excise map that has been requested by the market for many years, i.e. a timetable for tax changes, for the years 2023 to 2027. It is through this map that changes will be made in a predictable and gradual manner. The excise map will cover excise duties on ethyl alcohol, beer, wine, fermented beverages and intermediate products by 5% each year. and for cigarettes, smoking tobacco, cigars and cigarillos, novelty products and tobacco droughts by increasing the quota rate each year by 10%. How will these changes affect prices? They will not lead to drastic increases in the price of the drugs, but at the same time will achieve the objective of halting the trend of their increasing price availability. I would like to remind the High House that Eurostat data shows that consumption prices for alcoholic beverages in Poland are at one of the lowest levels in the European Union. Cheaper alcohol can only be bought in Bulgaria, Romania and Hungary. The price of a pack of cigarettes is lower than in Poland only in Bulgaria. A few numbers. After the changes in force in 2022, the taxation of a 0.75-litre bottle of wine will increase by around 16 gr, cigarette packages - by about 30 gr, 0.5-litre bottle of vodka - by about 1.5 PLN, while 0.5-litre bottle of beer - by only 6 gr. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Reducing the consumption of drugs and wider products that harm health by reducing their price availability is not only recommended by the OECD and the WHO, but also effective. We've experienced it ourselves. It is estimated that, as a result of the introduction of the sugar levy only in the first half of this year, sales of carbonated beverages decreased by 20% and that the bottled vodka below 300 ml - up to 1/3. I would like to remind you that smoking remains one of the main causes of death in Poland, i.e. approx. 67 thousand. cases per year. Each year, for reasons directly and indirectly related to the use of alcohol, 10 and 12 thousand die in Poland. persons. Worse still, the high number of teenagers reaching for cigarettes is worrying. About 15% of them smoked cigarettes at least once in the last 30 days. These are real challenges that will be addressed by the proposed change. The effect of indexing excise duties will result in an increase in budget revenue next year and in subsequent years by about PLN 2 billion per year. These are measures that will help to achieve the goal of increasing health spending over 6 years - up to 7% GDP by 2027 With this in mind, I kindly ask the High House to agree to the proposal as proposed by the Government. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u79	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present to the High House my position on the bill, 1612 and 1646. The first reading of the Parliamentary draft law on facilitating trade on Saturdays and Sundays by farmers and their household took place at the meeting of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on 12 October 2021. As a result of the work, the committee amended the draft Print Act No 1612 and forwarded to the High House the draft Print Law No 1646. The amendment concerns statutory trading days for farmers and their households. The record of Saturdays and Sundays has been changed to Fridays and Saturdays. Thus, the title of the proposed law was amended, which was replaced by the following: "The Act on the facilitation of trade on Fridays and Saturdays by farmers and their household." This project deals with the regulation of rules on trade between farmers and their households, which will contribute to better access for consumers to the possibility of buying food or artistic crafts from farmers in one designated place. The solutions proposed in the draft will help to shorten and diversify the distribution channels of local agri-food products, as well as to increase the incomes of family farms. In the draft law, the definition of agri-food products and handicrafts was established on the basis of the provisions of the Act of 21 December 2000, Dz. U., 2001 poz. 630. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 3 (1) (a) 1 of the proposed law indicates that the trade carried out by farmers and households is carried out in designated places by local authorities under the resolutions adopted by individual municipal councils. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 3 (1) (a) 2 It is specified that the designated place of trade shall comply with the conditions laid down in the provisions and comply with sanitary, veterinary, fire and construction requirements. An important factor is that the places designated by the municipalities' councils are characterized by convenient communication and proximity to the locality centre of a given village, and that other arguments related to, for example, are taken into account. with tourist destinations of the region concerned. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: The 4th draft law gives the municipalities the legitimacy to lay down rules on trade between farmers and their household. In the draft law pursuant to Article. 6 shall be exempt from the market fee of farmers and their household who, in accordance with the Council's resolution, trade in the designated place. The proposed law responds to the expectations of farmers and consumers who want to supply agri-food products directly to farmers, which will translate into the achievement of the objectives set by the designers. It is about enabling farmers and their households to trade in local authorities' designated places. The draft law is compatible with European Union law and is therefore not subject to the notification procedure. Law and Justice supports this proposal and will vote in favour of it by the High House. Thank you kindly. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u85	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present on behalf of the Civic Coalition a position on the draft law on facilitating trade on Saturdays and Sundays, and in fact today we already know that on Fridays and Saturdays. First of all, we believe that creating convenient conditions for Polish farmers, including small processors, to be able to sell directly their products, food products, is a directionally very good solution, tightens up and fits well into the strategy of the whole European Commission: from the field to the table, and today every product from production to the table reaches an average distance of about 170 km. The products of Polish farmers are mostly organic agricultural products, excellent quality products. At the level of ideas and ideas we fully agree, while at the level of implementation of this law we do not agree. We have a number of key solutions, we want this law and this idea to work in practice. First of all, we believe that it is not possible to charge every municipality with the obligation to create places of sale or markets, because for decades, and often hundreds of years, these places have established a custom. It is often the case that farmers have been bringing their products to a town or city for decades and have never sold their products to their neighbours in the neighbouring village in any way, just because the municipality is typically rural and dispersed. We therefore believe that every municipality must not be required to do so. Because what to do, for example. with the municipality of Platerówka in Lower Silesia, which has 2,5 or 3 thousand. The residents? It's impossible. It is therefore not an obligation, but a recommendation for municipalities to create this type of place. It's first of all to make it effective. Secondly, we don't think you can impose Fridays and Saturdays. We believe that in all the days in which such places operate, the Polish farmer should be exempt from the charges and be able to do so. If e.g. In some municipalities, the market space or market space is the place of trade, and this takes place on Tuesday and Thursday, it needs to be sanctioned. If trade is carried out somewhere from Monday to Saturday, the Polish farmer should be exempt from the charge for all these days and decide himself when he will arrive if this system is to work. Thirdly, we believe that any council of the municipality which chooses such a way of trade should consult, first, with the county authorities and the voivodship. There should be a regional and local coordinator, because you can't do anything against it. Municipalities often need to communicate on the place where trade will take place, and we know that often small rural communities want to do so e.g. in the town-rural commune. In this regard, consultation will be needed, including consultation, with agricultural chambers, with associations working for production, to promote trade and agricultural products for farmers. Here's another thing. We believe that if this is to be a really effective task and Polish citizens are to have a direct opportunity to purchase the product of Polish farmers, i.e. mainly organic, healthy food, thanks to this also cheaper food, because without a whole chain of subsequent companies that actually earn on margins, it needs to be created properly. What do we mean by that? We understand that the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, which in Poland is responsible for promoting agriculture and products, should be involved in all this, there should be a financial line that will provide assistance in the creation of new markets, financial support for additional jobs, to promote above all such trade. If this is to be effective, it cannot be done by the forces of the municipalities, it must also be done by the forces of all organizations, including the ARiMR and the funds that the ARiMR has to do so. Let's think about this law not in an ideal way, but in a practical way, because it's not going to work out, it's going to work out in a practical way. In principle, we all agree that Poles have and should be able to buy directly products produced by farmers, including small processors in the countryside. So we ask that these amendments be accepted if this law is to be effective in implementation. If these amendments do not pass, I think it will be impossible. Before the third reading, we will consider the amendments proposed today by the Civic Coalition Club. And this project is going in part in this direction. Therefore - realizing that he will not revolutionize the whole situation in Polish agriculture, but may have some positive effect - we will support this project. Incidentally, while we are at these trends, it is worth trying to try to reverse this dominance of online shops, supermarkets, hypermarkets, food discounts by shaping consumer patriotism on the one hand, and, on the other hand, by encouraging Poles with promotional actions to make purchases also in other places. Formula markets, bazaars or specially separated zones in smaller or larger cities, where farmers - but attention, farmers, not e.g. intermediaries impersonating these farmers - they could come and sell their products directly, require promotional support. People need to be shown the advantages of such a form of trade and create conditions for it. I don't know if these trends can be reversed. They are world trends observed both in Western European countries and in North American countries or even in Asia, where marketplace trade has always had a dominant position in trade. However, it is also worth taking care of this form of distribution through promotional activities, because the fact that there will be separate places by the mobs and mayors in cities where farmers will be able to come and sell their products seems to be a little short. And it's not enough for farmers to be exempt from the market fee when they sell these products. Of course, we will not solve this in this law, it has a broader dimension, but it is worth, by being part of European strategies, which recently, literally a few days ago, the European Parliament's resolution was adopted by a crushing majority of European parliamentarians - to try to adapt it through promotional measures into national law. Today, as a club of the Polish Coalition, we have submitted a bill on zero VAT for safe Polish healthy food. And this is part of the strategy that other groups and clubs should also support - strategies to promote Polish food, which today already meets the requirements of the European Green Deal, because it is most often produced with less chemical use than food in other countries of the European Union. And the best way to promote is to promote by the pocket. It is worth eliminating, abolishing tax on Polish food, this healthy, safe Polish food, for which the consumer pays by 7%, 5% more in the form of VAT. If this VAT had been abolished, this food would have been cheaper and would have been a direct benefit for the consumer in the form of cheaper purchases and, on the other hand, a preference for other foods that would have been imported. Therefore, it is worth not only supporting this project, but also - when our project shows up at a zero rate for safe Polish food - to support that project. These will then be complementary, comprehensive measures to improve the situation of Polish farmers, their smaller group, as in this case, or a larger group, as in the case of zero VAT rates for safe Polish food. These are good solutions for them and it is worth supporting them. However, this project, as I said at the outset, receives our support and we will support it in the vote. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u155	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Left Club, I am pleased to present my opinion on the draft amendment to the law on the system of development institutions, print no. 1632. The amendments to the Act on the System of Development Institutions aim to extend the possibilities of financing and scope of operation of the Polish Development Fund, among others. by implementing government programmes to address the effects of the COVID-19 epidemic. In the assumptions made by the government concerning the so-called. the PFR crisis shield programme is listed as one of the main actors responsible for the implementation of the PFR programme. The Polish Development Fund has become an entity, a company carrying out a public mission. It should be noted that under the rules in force prior to the entry into force of the amendment to the Polish Development Fund it could have been entrusted with the performance of tasks by government administrations. The government project has greatly expanded the competences of the PFR. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 21a The Act on the system of development institutions, which had already been passed, not today's procedure, enabled the Council of Ministers to entrust the PFR with the implementation of a government programme of providing financial support to entrepreneurs in particular in forms of holding or acquiring shares, shares, subscription warrants, bonds, claims, as well as accession to partnerships, loans, guarantees and guarantees, also in non-refundable forms. This government programme sets out the conditions, duration and scope of the financial support to entrepreneurs, the way in which the financing plans are prepared and the reporting on their implementation. Given that the Act on the system of development institutions currently indicates only the keeping of separate records, as was mentioned, for the tasks related to the implementation of each programme without defining the rules for this separation, it would seem appropriate to apply solutions similar to those relating to the functioning of the records so-called. Special funds. The introduction proposed in the draft law requires the separation of the special fund for each government programme, but raises concerns about the availability of funds by the Council of Ministers. The question to be asked is: Do the proposed amendments not have a hidden objective of reducing the control of the flow of funds and the rules for providing financial support to businesses? Why am I asking this? In the Rules of Procedure, you wrote that within the framework of the 2.0 program The PFR may allocate up to 20% of the funds between the amount allocated to micro-enterprises and the amount allocated to small and medium-sized enterprises after having agreed to change the allocation of funds by the Council of Ministers in the form of a resolution, so there is a great possibility of manoeuvring and changing the resources. Was there, therefore, such a change in the allocation of funds? If so, please submit a copy of the resolution of the Council of Ministers on this matter. The Polish Development Agency has far-reaching competence in implementing government support and assistance projects. However, it only operates under the supervision of the competent minister and supervisory board. Should not the principle of additional controls commissioned by the government of programmes be introduced? I believe that these tasks should be particularly subject to additional scrutiny and analysis by the Sejm of the Republic of Poland, i.e. committees and subcommittees, at least once a year or during the implementation of the project and shortly after its completion. As regards the substance of the draft law at issue on the amendment of the law on the system of development institutions, the proposed amendment is fully justified and, following the discussion of the club, we believe that it should be adopted. Thank you very much.

I would like to start by reminding you that the regulations contained in it have two fundamental objectives. The project increases the level of the upper limit of the threat of fines from the previous amount of 5 000. PLN, which operates until the mid-1990s of the last century, up to the amount of 30 thousand. zł in the case of a catalogue of traffic offences, which are defined in Chapter 11 of the Code of Offences, marked as offences against security and order in communication. In the current regulation, I will remind you, the principle is that a fine of up to PLN 500 may be imposed in the procedure of the ticket procedure and, in the case of coincidence, up to PLN 1 000. zł. This regulation, which is worth noting, has not been de facto corrected since the mid-1990s of the last century. Paragraph 24 1a Code of Offences. High Seym! In addition, in order to eliminate doubts, the following Article was adopted. 1 § 1a add reference to Article. Paragraph 24 1a to make it clear that only the offences referred to therein will be subject to the maximum amount of the fine, i.e. Up to 30,000. zł. This is the context that needs to be shown as a sphere of very severe effects that exist in social space, resulting from previous accident statistics in Poland, which is of course improving. I think that the regulation contained in the draft will significantly change this situation, that it is going in the right direction. The aim is to work together to work out a solution, above all by looking forward to processes for improving road safety, training process, testing of new drivers, etc. I would like to refer to the key amendments that have been examined and which the committee adopted during its meeting on 28 October, namely yesterday. In the assessment of the Infrastructure Committee, they shall primarily ensure consistency and detail of the proposed solutions. The amendments were also the result of a legislative discussion during the work of the subcommittee. These changes also make certain deadlines for the entry into force of the various provisions more consistent and, as it is worth noting, respond to the comments and recommendations made in the course of the work by both the Legislative Office and, subsequently, after examination by the Ministry of Infrastructure, also in the context of the comments of the Chief Medical Council and the Ombudsman, as I mentioned earlier. With regard to the amendments tabled, the provisions presented are clarified in such a way that they do not raise doubts of interpretation. I stress that this is also a novelty of legislative matter, because in this respect it has not been revised for more than 25 years, and some of the proposals in the project are also innovative because of technological issues and meet current and future road safety needs. The amendments tabled - those adopted yesterday - and I will now refer to them - aimed, first, at detailing the data on the payment of fines imposed by a criminal mandate. They can be supplemented in the Central Register of Drivers automatically by an electronic system that supports the records. This is due to discussions at the earlier meeting of the Emergency Sub-Committee and the comments of the Legislative Bureau. Secondly, they were intended to include so-called behaviours in the assessment. a young driver of the new types of offences contained in the Code of Offences for committing acts relevant to road safety and the need and need to make the catalogue of offences more consistent with the rules on the retention of a driving licence so-called. A young driver. Thirdly, we have further detailed the provisions proposed in the original submission from print No 1504 in the Road Traffic Law and the Driving Vehicle Act which provided the basis for the retention of the driver's driving licence, which does not comply with certain rules on the use of driver cards or record sheets and the use of tachographs, so that the possibility of stopping the driving licence is limited to cases of violations detected during road inspections. It is also a clear indication that it is only a matter of situations where drivers are obliged to use tachographs, including the recording of indications regarding the speed of the driver's activity or the distance travelled. In addition, the conditions of liability regarding the use of an unauthorised device or object have been supplemented by the use of modified software which may also prevent or cause counterfeiting or processing of data recorded by the tachograph. I mentioned earlier issues relating to technological aspects. In addition, it was recognised that a fine of up to 30 000 should be included in the catalogue of offences at risk of being fined. zł misdemeanor, which is referred to in Article. 96 § 3 of the Code of Offences, which is not to be indicated contrary to the obligation of the competent authority, upon request, to whom the vehicle is assigned to drive or use at the prescribed time. This is a misdemeanor, according to the article. Point 7 (2) of the draft law amending the Code of Conduct for Offences in the field of mandate proceedings in the cases referred to in Article. 96 § 3 of the Code of Offences, you may impose a penalty of up to 8 thousand. zł. This is the new proposal contained in the amending act. Failure to include this offence in the catalogue contained in the draft article. 24 § 1a of the Code of Offences would mean that in proceedings before a court, a fine could be imposed only up to 5 000. zł, i.e. lower than in the term of office. In view of the importance of refusing to indicate to whom the vehicle was entrusted to drive or use within the prescribed time limit, it was appropriate to include the offence in that Article. 93 § 3 of the Code of Offences to the catalogue of offences at risk of higher fines, i.e. up to a maximum of 30,000. zł. However, the time has come for reflection and much has been changed during the subcommittee meeting, during the committee meeting yesterday. These are changes that are needed and important. Of course, every one of us in this room should care and care that safety in the country is as high as possible, including driver safety, pedestrian safety, but also it cannot be a country based solely on prohibitions, orders and draconian penalties. Because other than what's been corrected... We fully agree that it is necessary to punish those who get behind the wheel after drinking alcohol after eating other medicines with similar effects. We agree that it is necessary to punish those who are disbarred from driving and who nevertheless get behind the wheel, who act irresponsiblely behind the wheel, which can be disastrous and sometimes is. But we also need to think multi-directionally, apart from penalties, increased parking tickets, for which, of course, drivers who mostly use cars, often work professionally, leave, go for a few hours, are tired, overworked. This also needs to be controlled: working conditions, loading conditions for trucks. So what if a short driving time is recorded on the tachograph, since the driver waits for a few hours to load or unload at the loading or unloading site. These are very important things. The same applies to roads that are being rebuilt. It is a good thing that these measures will be used to rebuild roads, but at this point I would call for a search of roads that have not been repaired, rebuilt, and to improve safety in places where accidents often occur. There are statistics. There are still problems with road crossings where accidents occur, where the surface often leaves much to be desired. Of course, everything can be explained and said that if you drive slowly, there will be no incident. But it's hard to imagine that we're going to be traveling more miles at 10 km/h, 20 km/h or 30 km/h. It's good in local traffic as we move temporarily. I have driven since Saturday, from morning, 1700 km - thank God, happily - and I try to drive according to regulations. On the other hand, we also need to look at those who drive professionally, who work to support whole families. And this is where I want us all to have an impact on that awareness. Training, meetings - it's all very important. Because the imposed huge mandate, the imposed punishment will not only scare, although of course it will result, but will also cause the big money to have to find those who often do not have the money. I can't agree with the fact that when there are very high fines, accidents will not occur because there are many factors that are independent of us: getting the trailer out of the car, leaving the machine on the road. There's more and more of these cases. Of course, we do not know what two amendments have been tabled by Mr Law and Justice at the moment, so we will take a decision before the vote. We'll try to vote with common sense. Thank you very much. [[Piotr Zgorzelski, Vice-Marshal of the Sejm, is the chairman of the proceedings.]]

This is one thing in our assessment that will improve the execution, and the other is the possibility of downloading funds from the account of such a person in a situation where he has overpaid PIT tax and in the annual account this overpayment would be in his account. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u267	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the pleasure and the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Coalition Poland - PSL, UED, Conservatives on the Senate bill amending the Act - Code of Civil Procedure. This draft law defines the animals and objects belonging to the farmer holding the agricultural holding which are not subject to enforcement and which have so far been regulated by the provisions of the Regulation of the Minister of Justice of 5 July 2017 on the identification of items belonging to the farmer holding which are not subject to enforcement. Secondly, this concerns the repeal of the Article. 830 Law of 17 November 1964 - Code of Civil Procedure. According to the new draft law, the basic herd of livestock is not subject to enforcement. I mean dairy cattle, meat cattle, horses, goats, sheep, pigs, poultry, fur animals, and livestock outside the basic herd, which are in the second half of the gestation period and during the breeding period of the offspring, as well as basic machinery and equipment, agricultural tools necessary for farm work, grain silos and feed silos, fuel stocks, fuel supply for 6 months, fertilisers and plant protection products, economic buildings and agricultural land necessary for livestock farming. In addition, the bailiff shall, within 3 days following the seizure, apply to the farmhouse competent for the location of the debtor's holding within 21 days of the opinion referred to in paragraph 1. Until an opinion is delivered, the bailiff shall suspend enforcement of surplus inventory and items. The legislator, among others. This is why it treats agriculture differently by applying specific regulations. It is not enough for this part of the economy to be objective, but also for a political factor to change the law that is in line with the agricultural policy. On the other hand, enforcement plays an important role in ensuring legal order, forcing compliance with the obligations laid down in the decisions of the competent authorities or directly in the rules. On the other hand, its effects may lead to significant personal and economic problems, difficulties and inconveniences on the part of the obliged party, taking into account this part of the economy. Protection of an active farmer and his family is therefore important not only because of his agricultural activity but also because he is a supplier of public goods. The problems of farmers in fulfilling their obligations often do not arise from risky or wrong economic decisions, and for reasons entirely independent of them, the fulfilment of the obligations imposed by society. In the light of Art. 23 The Constitution is justified by the special treatment of agriculture in order to take account of the increased risk of agricultural production and to assume a part of the public's responsibility for agricultural activities in a special way. It must be accepted that, in the light of constitutional requirements, all exemptions from executions relating to animals and movable goods needed to run a farm must be regulated at the statutory level. They are currently regulated by the Regulation of the Minister of Justice on the identification of items belonging to the farmer holding which are not subject to enforcement. The regulation is a normative act which can only serve to implement the law and not replace it, supplement it or modify the solutions adopted in it, and therefore the proposed amendments are justified. The transfer to the Law of Solutions which have so far been contained in the Regulation of the Minister of Justice of 5 July 2017 on the identification of items belonging to the farmer holding the holding which are not subject to enforcement, in substance which almost reflects the provisions contained in the Regulation, in addition to the terminology and editorial corrections, we consider it reasonable and necessary, given the need to ensure the transparency and regularity of the position of the legal standards in this regard. Furthermore, we agree that the extension of the deadline for the opinion given by the competent agricultural authority on the appropriateness (the bell) of the exclusion from enforcement of surplus livestock or items in excess of the provisions of the Civil Procedure Code should be considered to be very valid from 14 to 21 days and, therefore, such opinion of the Agricultural Chamber is necessary in this regard. Parliamentary Club Polish People's Party, UED, Conservatives will vote in favour of this bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u297	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! It must be agreed that, in the light of constitutional requirements, any exemption from animal enforcement, the movable goods needed to run a holding must be regulated at the statutory level and, consequently, the proposed amendments restore the merit of constitutionality. This positive opinion on the project has already been expressed in the Government's position, and some comments have also been made to stimulate the applicant's reflection on issues such as: the need to introduce arrangements allowing the bailiff, in justified cases, to appoint an expert to verify the findings of the Chamber's opinion. During the work on this project, a broad and substantive discussion on the project, including representatives of the Minister of Justice, took place at the meeting of the subcommittee for the revision of civil law. I would like to thank you for this substantive, good discussion. Two amendments were tabled during the meeting. The first amendment was intended to improve the opinion of the Agricultural Chambers and to increase their substantive value by adding a copy of the minutes of seizure of the debtor's assets and information on the total amount of the debtor's claims. It was also decided that the opinion of the Agricultural Chamber does not constitute information within the meaning of Article. 761 §1 of the Civil Procedure Code. The second amendment allows bailiffs to verify the findings which are contained in the opinion of the agricultural chamber by establishing an expert for the permissible scope of occupation. There is no doubt that the amendments tabled will undoubtedly contribute to the smoother execution of farmers holding a holding who are debtors and, above all, to the execution in such a way as to protect those debtors, i.e. respectful of the principle of adequacy and humanitarianism. It is also worth mentioning with approval those amendments which were tabled at the Extraordinary Commission meeting on changes in codification. The extension of the vacathio legis is advisable due to the importance of the proposed changes and the clarification of the transitional provision will allow for faster application of the proposed amendments. With regard to the questions raised by your Members, I would like to point out the following. The draft law primarily deals with the identification of items belonging to the farmer holding the holding which are not subject to enforcement. It therefore does not seem appropriate, in these provisions, to also regulate the issues that you have raised regarding the exclusion from the execution of pet animals, which may have each of us. With regard to the issue of legislation, whether the Ministry of Justice is working on the issue of amending the rules on enforcement by bailiffs, I would like to point out that such conceptual and analytical work is being carried out on an ongoing basis in the department responsible for civil law legislation. Where the conclusions of this work are that specific provisions need to be amended, such an initiative is taken. As I have already pointed out - because there has been an allegation that this regulation was at the level of the Minister of Justice's regulation - so once again, it is good that this regulation has been transferred to the statutory level. Rules which are introduced in enforcement proceedings are always intended to civilise this procedure. As I said before, we are concerned with the well-being of the citizens. A citizen can become a potential debtor at any time due to different situations of life. Our work, which we are doing, is to civilize these principles, and we always take these circumstances into account. With regard to the decision which was referred to in the draft act as an expert decision - this opinion is to be the final opinion - I would like to explain that the experts have the necessary knowledge to make such a valuation. The expert's opinion is intended to clarify any doubts as to the value of the asset. Thank you very much.

In this context, the pace of proceedings over this law, Minister - I support what I said earlier - is unacceptable. You claim that the project was widely consulted with industry and local authorities. In recent days, however, associations and organisations and local governments have asked me and many parliamentarians to submit doubts and amendments to the project. It welcomes the fact that the government recognises the need for greater emphasis on electromobility, but apart from declarations and nice words, it is worth paying attention to real actions and numbers. High Izbo! In 2016, the then Deputy Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki and Minister of Energy Krzysztof Tchórzewski announced a plan on their way to electromobility, which announced that by 2025 1 million electric cars would be moving on Polish roads. There is the end 2021 and how many electric cars do we have in Poland? 27,000. Five years after the announcement, the government didn't even fulfill it in 3%. As the Civic Coalition, we will certainly watch closely the implementation of the provisions of this law. It should be stressed that electromobility is only one element of the process of transforming our energy and striving to achieve climate neutrality. The development of electromobility must go hand in hand with moving away from coal to renewable energy sources. The Civic Coalition in this area will be particularly vigilant by the rulers and remind them of the energy transition, which is crucial for development, not just economic, our country. The bill introduces many solutions long-awaited by local governments, entrepreneurs and industry organisations, such as the possibility of establishing clean transport zones in all municipalities regardless of their population - in the current law it was 100,000. residents. The bill also introduces the possibility of establishing clean transport zones, as I said, and facilitating procedures for the creation of charging points for electric vehicles on multi-family buildings in parking spaces in communities, housing cooperatives. The Civic Coalition has prepared amendments aimed at limiting the time of entry into clean transport zones for gas-powered vehicles by the end of 2026 and wishes to add provisions to enable local authorities to implement the infrastructure of public charging stations, as well as provisions to maintain the plan for building this infrastructure and to set a new deadline by 31 December 2023. As we know, Silesia is the only one who has dealt with this problem. It has not been dealt with in other regions of Poland. The draft law also contains provisions for the development of hydrogen infrastructure, introduces the principle of providing financial support to entrepreneurs in the purchase of vehicles, including through leasing, and implements three EU directives. This bill will contribute to the development of electromobility, the neutrality of climate transport. Therefore, as the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Coalition will support the proposed project. Mr. Marshal, I submit the amendments tabled. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Electric cars, 3,000. charging points. In accordance with the provisions of this Law, this was to be done by far more since 2018, but it did not work. Now I think that this bill partly responds to these problems, because of which it was not possible to achieve this. I think the key problem in all of this is the issue of infrastructure, so charging points. The Act adopts certain arrangements for increasing the number of charging points as regards the number of parking spaces and issues related to public charging points. However, the Act introduces a very important change, namely that distribution system operators, i.e. energy companies, will no longer be obliged to build charging points. In fact, this is a really good solution, because distribution system operators are not for that. They're more from powering these charging points, but not from building them. But here, of course, there is a problem that the Minister knows perfectly well and which we have discussed, so the question of settlement of those expenditures that have already been incurred and the question of settlement also of expenditures that have not been incurred, because we know very well that there are several companies that have not built any station. This, too, should be a reason for the rulers to draw consequences for the managers of these companies, since they do not comply with the laws to which they are obliged. So this issue of settlement will be very important. In this context, it will also be very important to transfer or sell this infrastructure to operators so that they can continue to build these stations. The next point is undoubtedly very important in this context, and also in the context - I see, Mr Suski - of the debate on photovoltaics. Today we know that this system can't stand it. We're talking about the energy system. Therefore, this is another problem that we are dealing with, namely the energy infrastructure that will supply these stations. So, undoubtedly, perhaps not in this law, perhaps in government activities, there should be facilitations that will somehow compensate for the financial expenditure on such investments. Another issue is the question of self-government. The authorities point out that okay is the question of the plans to which they were obliged, but the question is what will happen to these plans at the moment. I would like to remind you that these plans were a task commissioned by the state administration, so it would be good to also resolve these issues in this respect. The idea is that local authorities really have such an incentive to present these plans for the development of electromobility in a given municipality, and to also have financial resources to prepare these plans, and perhaps later to carry out the tasks that result. These are already the tasks of the National Environmental Fund and the State Administration - issues relating to increased funds for the purchase of low-carbon buses or electric cars for the administration. In conclusion, I would like to say that this bill is going in the right direction. And by drawing conclusions from what has been going on so far, I think the Left Club will vote in favour of this bill (the bell). Following the adoption of this law, the state of implementation of its provisions in terms of the development of electromobility should be monitored every six months. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u367	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Government's draft amendment to the Law on specific arrangements for the prevention, prevention and eradication of COVID-19, other communicable diseases and the crisis situations caused by them, and certain other laws, prints 1675 and 1679, introduces a comprehensive complement to the provisions on the settlement of benefits granted from the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund, as well as from the Labour Fund for the protection of jobs under State aid in response to the SARS-COV-2 epidemic. The Provincial Labour Offices were granted until 31 August this year. entrepreneurs and employers to finance employees' salaries for PLN 16 036 million for 110 670 entities. In order to prevent job losses during lockdown, there was a need to provide these benefits as soon as possible and therefore requests for them were based on the statements made by the operators that the required conditions were met. Due to the scale of the problem, the COVID provides for a period of 3 years for the final settlement of these benefits. The changes proposed in the draft amendment will facilitate the settlement of the funds transferred to employers. Clarification of the rules will benefit both entrepreneurs and voivodship or district labour offices, ZUS and other entities participating in the aid scheme. The changes will minimise the risk of different interpretations, narrow the need for recovery and reduce financial sanctions in such situations, and improve the control and clearance of grants. For example, the penalty will be reduced by the reimbursement of the remuneration in the event of termination of the employment contract with the employee. The employer will not have to repay all the aid, but only to finance the remuneration of the redundant worker. The project envisages, among others, relaxing the conditions for loans from the Labour Fund to start a business or create jobs for people and companies affected by the COVID crisis. The changes proposed in the Act on the Tourist Aid Fund will create more favourable conditions for the tourism industry, which has suffered greatly during the pandemic. The amendment will also enable the Minister for Labour to announce a programme of professional activation and social inclusion for foreigners and immigrants in particular difficult situations. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! At yesterday's meeting, the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy introduced a number of legislative amendments to the bill and adopted the text of the Act unanimously. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports and will vote in favour of this bill. At the same time, our club is proposing nine amendments aimed at further improvements in the process of clearing the benefits granted in defence of jobs, as well as reducing too restrictive, as was apparent from the committee's deliberations, the criminal provision. We hope to continue to work on this law over party divisions. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u368	Thank you very much, sir. Thank you. The position of the Parliamentary Club The Civic Coalition will be presented by Mrs Marta Wcisło. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u369	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Civic Coalition, I have the honour to present my position on the draft Print Act 1675 and 1679 together with the amendment. The Government's draft law amending the Law on Against and Combating COVID-19 introduces amendments to the Act of 2 March 2020, which mainly concern the rules for the clearing of subsidies received by entrepreneurs from the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund. These changes in the rules on the settlement of aid granted to the vast majority should be assessed positively. Currently, when reading the law literally, if an entrepreneur who has received assistance in the form of support for the maintenance of jobs not subject to economic downtime or reduced working time, he fired the worker for reasons not affecting him, this would result in the need for such an entrepreneur to repay all the aid received. Following the entry into force of the revised amendments, the reimbursement will only apply to the part of the co-financing which has been granted to the worker released and the reimbursement of the entire co-financing will only take place in the event of serious and far-reaching infringements. However, the proposal to add an article to the above law raises serious doubts. 31zzt and Art. 31zzu. They concern the Tourist Return Fund or the Tourist Aid Fund. They impose a high penalty in cases where a tourist operator does not pay instalments to the Tourist Return Fund or the Tourist Aid Fund or lowers the contribution to the Tourist Aid Fund. This punishment is imprisonment until the age of 3. It is also difficult not to note that the criminal sanctions imposed in the present case are an attempt to encourage entrepreneurs to pay their debts in the first place for the above mentioned. funds. However, it must be borne in mind that the Tourist Returns and Tourist Aid Fund has been set up in connection with the ongoing epidemic and is directly linked to it and is therefore of a specific nature and results from a specific need to reduce the effects of the COVID-19 epidemic. It is therefore not possible to introduce rules governing their functioning which are similar to those laid down in the Act of 24 November 2017, i.e. the provisions concerning the Tourist Guarantee Fund, which was created in times prior to the epidemic, in a completely different economic situation. These solutions cannot be transferred to a law created to reduce the effects of the epidemic, which has hit the tourism industry so seriously, in particular the seasonal ones. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Coalition hereby amends this amendment to remove from the procedated provisions amendments to the Law of 2 March 2020 on specific arrangements for the prevention, prevention and eradication of COVID-19, other communicable diseases and the crisis situations of Article. 31zzu and Art. 31zzt. Mrs. Marshal! I am making an amendment on behalf of the Civic Coalition. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u370	Thank you, Member. Mr Tadeusz Tomaszewski will speak on behalf of the Left Club.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u373	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! Within the framework of the benefits provided for in the Law of 2 March 2020 on specific arrangements for the prevention, prevention and eradication of COVID-19, other communicable diseases and crisis situations, hereinafter referred to as COVID-19, supporting entrepreneurs and other persons employing workers in connection with the crisis caused by the epidemic, it was granted inter alia. benefits from the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund for the protection of jobs in the form of subsidies for the remuneration of employees. In view of the unprecedented situation caused by the COVID-19 epidemic and the need to support operators in economic difficulties as soon as possible, the benefits were granted on the basis of applications which were based on declarations of fulfilment of the conditions for applying for such support. The scale of the planned and provided support by the voivodship labour offices led to the fact that the COVID-19 provides for the final settlement of benefits for the protection of jobs granted from the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund for a period of 3 years from the end of the period covered by the grant of the remuneration of employees for the beneficiary concerned. The proposed amendments to the COVID-19 law facilitate, in particular, the settlement of the funds transferred under the aid. Clarification of the rules will benefit entrepreneurs and other entities who have received funding for salaries for employees. By clarifying the provisions, these entities will have more knowledge of their legal situation in connection with obtaining aid and possible consequences in the absence of compliance with the obligations under that law. The clarification of the rules also minimises the risk of different interpretations and different decisions being taken by the authorities applying those rules, as well as by the courts. It will also have a positive impact on the improvement of these accounts, which is beneficial for the recipients. The beneficiaries will be better informed of the final settlement and the assurance that their settlement has been deemed to have been carried out correctly. The intention to amend the COVID-19 law was also very positively assessed by the voivodship labour offices, which indicated difficulties in applying the rules on accounting and reimbursement of funds in the current form. The proposed regulations were also consulted with voivodship labour offices. However, our concerns and concerns are raised by proposals to amend the rules on the functioning of the Tourist Aid Fund, where it is proposed to add the provision of the Article accordingly. 31zzt and Art. 31zzu act COVID-19. This proposal aims to impose sanctions on entrepreneurs for persistent non-payment of payments to the Tourist Return Fund or the Tourist Aid Fund, as well as sanctions for undermining the amount of the contribution due to the Tourist Aid Fund. The rules are intended to influence the discipline of entrepreneurs in respect of compliance with their obligations as regards the payment of the instalments or contributions due to them. The fund. The question therefore arises as to whether sanctions are in themselves the right way in the current situation of the COVID and whether the criterion of only two instalments is not too restrictive. The Polish Coalition - Polish People's Party Club, the Union of European Democrats and Conservatives will support this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The purpose of this regulation is to grant persons who have obtained the right to early retirement and are caring for children who require permanent care additional benefits to compensate for the amount of those pensions up to the level of care provision. This law regulates all that is needed in order to obtain this benefit. I think that's a very good step. Many families have been waiting for this. In fact, it was a high injustice when families sacrificed, usually mothers, but also fathers, to look after their children. As a result, they received a reduced pension. Right now, we have a chance to make up for this delay, this injustice. A big thank you to everyone who worked on this bill. It must also be said, as was the case with the previous proposal, that the discussion in the committee was indeed substantive and consistent. More opportunities like this. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u406	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and three-minute statements on behalf of the circles. I'm opening a discussion. The position of Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will be presented to the High House by Mrs Danuta Nowicka. Come on in.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u407	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the Parliamentary bill on compensation for early retirement for children requiring constant care, prints 1614, 1614-A and 1678. The purpose of the proposed regulation is to grant to persons entitled to early retirement under the Council of Ministers Regulation of 15 May 1989 on the entitlement to early retirement of child-care workers requiring permanent care, the provisions of which were in force until 31 December 1998, an additional allowance equalising the amount of those pensions up to the level of care provision. The draft law sets out the conditions for the acquisition of the right, the procedure for granting and the rules for the payment and financing of compensatory benefits for early retirement for children requiring permanent care. The purpose of the compensatory benefit is to provide additional income support to early retirement for care for children requiring constant care, so-called. EWK's pension. The pension of the EWK shall be granted regardless of the age of one of the parents with the required period of employment - 20 years for women and 25 years for men - under the employment contract and forced to stop this work due to the health condition of the child requiring constant care. The draft law proposes to grant to persons entitled to an EWK pension whose pension is lower than the amount of the care provision, compensatory benefit up to the amount of the care provision. The primary objective of both benefits is to provide means of subsistence for people who are caring for a disabled family member who are resigning from employment for this reason. The draft law proposes that, during the annual valorisation of pension benefits, EWK's pensions should not only be valued, but also increased if they are lower, to the amount of care provided in force from January of the year in question. The compensatory allowance will be granted at the request of the person entitled to the Social Insurance Institution or other pension body who pays the benefits. The compensatory allowance will be granted on a monthly basis in a gross amount corresponding to the difference between the amount of the care provision and the amount of the EWK pension. This draft Compensation Act applies only to a group of pensioners who, under the Council of Ministers Regulation of 15 May 1989 on the right to early retirement of child-care workers who require constant care, have obtained the right to the so-called. EWK pensions. The estimated number of currently entitled to an EWK pension is around 25 000. The expected cost of regulation is about PLN 165 million in 2022, financed by the state budget. The proposed regulation is very much needed and expected. The Law and Justice Club will vote in favour. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u409	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! In 2003 the Sejm passed a law on family benefits. A care provision has been introduced for parents who resign to care for a disabled child. The Act indicates that the benefit is not payable if the care provider has a fixed pension right. As a result, the duality of benefits was created, which was not gross until 2014, as early retirement and care were at a similar level. In 2014, after the protests, we introduced changes, thanks to which this care provision was linked to the rise in the lowest wage, which resulted in an increase and annual valorisation. Unfortunately, early retirement recipients were trapped, unable to give up their pension. Two further steps had to be taken: to compensate for early retirement and to compensate for the benefits for caretakers who acquired disability after 18 years of age. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u455	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I am pleased to present a report on the work of the Committee on Energy, Climate and State Assets concerning the draft law amending the Act - Energy Law, print no. 1674 and 1683. The aim of the proposed legislative amendments to this law is to reduce the effects of the increase in gas fuel prices that we are dealing with on the European market and in Poland, and to minimise these effects on Polish households. In recent weeks, gas prices have increased by almost 900% on freight exchanges. Many factors have contributed to these rapid increases. Overall, after the pandemic, demand for this fuel has increased in the European Union. Through the Jamal pipeline to western Europe in the first days of October only one quarter of what was received each month earlier. The reserves collected in warehouses located in Europe were at historically low levels, and during the summer period Gazprom and its related companies emptied their own warehouses located in several Western European countries instead of complementing the level of stocks. During the summer period, the pandemic delayed maintenance work on pipelines and reduced supplies. There was less wind than usual in Western Europe and less water energy, which reduced the available amount of renewable energy. The price of carbon dioxide emissions is rising rapidly and steadily, but at this point it must be stressed that the multiple increases in gas prices have been influenced by an informed Russian policy which thus wants to put pressure on Europeans to act as soon as possible. Nord Stream 2. This, unfortunately, helps her to take a very careless approach of the Western European countries to Kremlin's policy, which was warned by President Andrzej Duda and Prime Minister Morawiecki, as well as by MEPs of Law and Justice. Russia, specifically Gazprom, has reduced deliveries. This company doesn't even hide its true intentions. One vice president said that the situation could be improved when Nord Stream 2 obtains certification without European legislation. One of the reasons for the rise in gas prices is the announcement of the radicalisation of climate policy as expressed in 13 drafts of legislation consisting of the Fit for 55 package presented by Commissioner Timmermans. The increase in gas prices was also influenced by the increased demand of Chinese people who bid above market rates and buy further charges in tenders. Despite such a difficult situation on the gas market, the Commissioner for Energy of the European Union stated that the best response to price challenges was to speed up the achievement of the 65% target. the share of renewable electricity in the EU mix by 2030 High Izbo! As we know, Poland buys gas mainly from Russia under the Jamal contract with the Russian Gazprom concern, which is valid until 2023. This adverse agreement concluded by previous governments means that Poland must either import gas from a Russian supplier or pay contractual penalties for not receiving it. Hence, among others. the advantage of supplies from Russia. Now we also have contracts for the supply of gas from Qatar, Norway and the USA. The rise in gas prices can be a negative result, and high inflation can be sustained. In Poland we had an increase in gas prices in mid-September. At that time, the Energy Regulatory Office approved a 7.4% increase in tariffs. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! At yesterday's session of the Parliament's Committee on Energy, Climate and State Assets, Members worked on a draft law that provided for the possibility to spread gas prices to households over time. According to the provisions of the Act, the increase in gas purchase prices during the tariff period will be transferable to subsequent tariff periods. Such a mechanism could be used for 3 years so that there are no jumping increases. The proposed rules allow for minimising the increase in gas prices for the most vulnerable groups of customers, namely households, while ensuring that their sellers can recover their costs at a later date. The Energy Law novel prepared by the PiS Group assumes that gas vendors whose tariffs are approved by the President of URE will be able to submit for approval a tariff taking into account the costs of purchasing gas planned during its validity by 30 June 2022. If, at that time, gas purchase costs increase beyond planned costs, the seller will be able to recover them in subsequent tariffs that will apply from the beginning of 2023; according to the project, it will calculate these costs in a specific manner and they will be considered justified. To be included in subsequent tariffs, the seller will be 3 years old from the beginning of 2023. According to the project, the increase in the next tariff, established on the basis of the proposed regulation, during the 12 months following the period of application of the tariff to which an adjustment for the increase in gas purchase prices is made shall not exceed 25% the level of the average price that would have been established without accounting for this adjustment. The introduction of this regulation will prevent the risk of one large and cumulative increase in gas tariffs for 2022. Applicants indicate that tariffs approved to date for 2022 provide for around 100% , and in some cases even more than 100% raises. The draft allows the possibility of correcting tariffs and tariff applications submitted before the date of entry into force of the Act. To this end, the sellers concerned shall submit appropriate requests for corrections within 14 days of the date of entry into force. It should be stressed that the proposed novel will not be mandatory. It will be optional for businesses supplying gas to households. The solution does not cost the state budget or the budgets of local government units. The draft rules raised the debate and questions of Members at the committee meeting yesterday. Some of them even requested that the government decide to reduce the tax burden associated with energy raw materials, e.g. excise duty and VAT on gas. Finally, the draft amendment was supported by a committee with 18 votes in favour and seven abstentions. Previously, several amendments were made to the draft, which were suggested by Parliamentary legislators. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Commission for Energy, Climate and State Assets requests the High Sejm to adopt a draft law - Energy Law, print no. 1674, which aims to prevent sudden increases in gas prices for households. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

High Izbo! Freedom of assembly is a right which belongs to every citizen of a democratic rule of law. Freedom of assembly is a category of first-generation human rights, as is the right to personal freedom and security, the right to legal protection, the right to freedom of religion. Freedom of assembly shall be determined by Article. 57 Constitution: Everyone is given the freedom to organise and participate in peaceful assembly. It is the constitution that guarantees freedom of political, public and private assemblies related to the sphere of public life. Also art. 32 of the Constitution speaks of the principle of equality between the citizen and the law and that no one can be discriminated against in political, economic life for any reason. I will also mention that the Constitutional Tribunal has spoken many times on the issue of assembly, stressing that the assembly is an element of democracy and determines the exercise of other freedoms and rights relating to the sphere of public life. This is the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 28 June 2000, Journal of Laws 2000 No 53 item. 694. Similarly, the Constitutional Tribunal in its judgment of 18 September 2014 underlines the fact that one of the functions of assembly is the function of protecting the rights of minorities. The Constitutional Tribunal referred to the function of stability of freedom of assembly in its judgment of 18 January 2006. However, as far as the historical dimension is concerned, I would like to stress that Aristotle has already defined the concept of right to assembly, defining: who in a country has the right to participate in assemblies and tribunals is called a citizen of that State. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u629	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! It's been 45 years. On 23 September 1976, the opposition group appealed to the public and the authorities of the Polish People's Republic and set up a Committee for the Defence of Workers. The appeal was signed by 14 members of the founders of the KOR, among others. Jerzy Andrzejewski, Stanisław Barańczak, Jacek Kuroń, Jan Józef Lipski, Antoni Macierewicz. The Committee consisted of people with differing political views and different intellectual backgrounds. Next to each other there were Catholics and socialists: Antoni Macierewicz and Jacek Kuroń. Among the activists were young people and activists who still remembered the times of the partitions, the Polish Socialist Party. Openness, flexibility, true ideological desire to help, and hence the ability to work beyond divisions - were the strength of the NRA. The main objective of the organisation was to protect victims of repression associated with suppression of the so-called. June events, i.e. workers' speeches in Ursus, Radom and Płock. The NRA's activists made demands to accept redundant participants in speeches, to release those imprisoned for their participation in strikes, to reveal the extent of repression, to punish those responsible for violating the law. Helping families detained and convicted of strikes was not only about financial support, but also about access to information, the creation of a list of detained or legal aid. KOR's activities to help the victims included activists from the Warsaw Scout Team, Antoni Macierewicz, Piotr Naimski, Wojciech Onyszkiewicz, members of the Warsaw Catholic Intelligence Club, among others. Henryk Wujec, oppositionists, activists from March 1968, Jacek Kuroń, Adam Michnik, intellectuals Edward Lipiński, Jan Józef Lipski. Following the achievement of its main objective, the Committee for the Defence of Workers turned into the Committee for Social Self-Defense of the KOR, which operated until the creation of the NSZZ.Solidarity. The KOR was one of the most important opposition organizations in the Polish People's Republic until the establishment of the Solidarity Fund. May the memory of these people in this room never have different messages. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Bolesław Chrobre in Piotrków Trybunalski. This building is the first High School to teach them. Bolesława Chrobrego, one of the oldest secondary schools in Poland. For most plums, it's just the Chrobry, a school you talk about either with admiration and pride if you're ending it, or with jealousy and a snack, if you don't have that privilege. After the failure of trying to open a school - a college in Krakow in the middle of the 17th century, the order of drunkards became interested in Piotrków, the city in which the Crown Court for Wielkopolska was established. The Order gained the support of the Piotrków authorities and already in 1675 in this city began to operate a drunken school. The competition for drunkards was Jesuits who also intended to open a school in Piotrków. However, despite obtaining the consent of John III Sobieski it was opened long after his death, because in 1706. The Jesuits built their school, going beyond the defensive walls. Until the end of the convent today Chrobry gained part of the walls, the main ground floor and the first floor. In 1769 the building was taken over by the National Education Commission, the world's first Ministry of National Education, and in 1781, it was handed over to drunkards who built the second floor of the school. When after the second partition of Piotrków Trybunalski became the capital of the so-called. South Prussia, the former college became a POW camp, a prison. The Prussians finally finished building the building. The last floor is still a disorder of proportion and lack of harmony. After 1815, it was operating in the Urban Government Secondary School of Chrombry. Not only were young men from all over the Russian Empire taught it, but it also taught it to young people from all over the Russian Empire. Students of middle school paid tribute to blood in the November and January Uprisings. In 1905, they fought for the Polish speech, which was perfectly written by Dr. Zygmunt Tenenbaum in his magnificent Gagady about the old Piotrków. During World War II most of the school's equipment was destroyed and the Germans organized a military hospital in the building. The school teachers, despite the repression of the occupier, unconditionally began to work in secret teaching. The students of Chrobry enthusiastically engaged in the independence conspiracy after 1945. The Soyczyński organization called the Polish Conspiracy Army was arrested and imprisoned in the UB Chambers. Leopold Bucket paid the highest price for his love of his homeland. Scouts from the Piotrków gymnasium established in 1949 the Small Diversion organization, which was developed by the Security Office in November 1950. Then the legendary director of Chrobry Theophile Budzanowski was arrested. Chrobry was the first high school in the region, where a real professional planetarium was created thanks to the determination of Prof. Zbigniew Solarz. For more than 20 years the High School and High School Education Society has been in operation and since 1992 the school museum has been operating. The list of famous Chrobrego graduates is long and there is no time to list all those characters who graduated from this school. Just mention Józef Pawlikowski, Stanisław Rembek, Filip Sulimierski, Mieczysław Trajdos, Stefan Rowecki and many others. This school is a bridge between history and the future. Her graduates have a chance to gain an education that will allow them to enter adult life with the necessary resources of information. Chrobry's a really big school. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u684	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! All Saints Day, Day of the Dead. November is the month of memory of those who have passed away from us. Especially on November 1, most Poles visit cemeteries, burial sites of the dead. According to the definition, a cemetery is an institutionally shaped section of a space with a programmed burial purpose, organized according to certain directives, cultural rules related to the ritualization of forms of burial of the dead, as well as the existence of a certain tradition, the preservation of memory of them. The name comes from the Latin word coemeterium of this very meaning, which in turn is a Latinized form of the Greek word meaning the place of rest, from the word to sleep. The Jewish cemetery is a kirkut, while the Muslim cemetery is a mizar. The idea of the cemetery as an inviolable and holy space took early Christianity from the Roman tradition, so the first Christian cemeteries separated themselves from former pagan cemeteries. The church already in the third century gave them under the care of bishops and priests, creating a system of cemetery administration. The cemeteries were then holy places and protected by special immunity. They also included the right of asylum. Ancient or early Christian cemeteries located near cities or other old cemeteries with a large area, especially those where members of known families and famous people are buried, are usually called necropolises. This term is also used for a modern or even modern cemetery surrounded by a particular cult. What's today? Walking through cemeteries, looking for names and names, let us also think of those who no longer exist in the world, who also have no families. Novák na Neuman, from Neuman to Nowok, from Nowok to Neuman, and then suddenly again to Polish Nowak. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-28-sejm-40-1.u706	Dear Prime Minister, At the meeting of the subcommittee on the parliamentary draft law amending the Act on Renewable Energy Sources, which took place on 15 October, a number of amendments were introduced, which radically changed the way Prosumer Energy works since the new year. Today's prosumers transmit excess energy produced to the electricity grid to collect later on 70% to 80% of the given surplus free of charge. I would like to remind you that the government's draft amendment to this law, which appeared in June this year, assumed a complete elimination of wasteland and caused great concern on the PV market. The emergence of an alternative draft amendment to the RES Act introduced by the Group of Members gave the hope of reaching a compromise between the necessary changes and the expected benefits of future prosumers. In July of this year, the possibility of introducing a transition period based on 1:1 waste for all users of home photovoltaics connected to the network by the end of 2023 was considered, in accordance with the original assumptions of the parliamentary amendment to the RES Act. The changes made to the project completely ignore the net-metering mechanism 1:1 and replace it so-called. net billing. Thus, from the government project, a negative solution for prosumers was transferred to the proceded parliamentary project, printing No 1382, completely changing its assumptions. Thus, contrary to previous announcements, the 1:1 net-metering system will not apply from 1 January 2022 and new users of photovoltaics will only benefit from the current rules for six months and then from 1 July the machine will be subject to a negative sales system, net-billing. According to net-billing rules, excess energy produced by new prosumers is to be sold to the grid at wholesale prices and the missing energy will have to be redeemed at retail prices - usually about twice as high. Importantly, the wholesale purchase price of electricity from PV installations would be variable, after 2024 - determined on an hourly basis, i.e. in the long term practically unpredictable. The EU directive talks about changing clearing systems after 31 December 2023, which means that the government is more than 2 years old to make changes. The pace of work adopted by the Ministry on these changes is highly harmful to society and causes a sense of injustice in those who do not have time to start their photovoltaic installations before 1 January 2022. The accompanying changes in haste raise many questions, including: on the outcome of the public consultation for the government's draft RES amendment. I would therefore ask for information on the outcome of the public consultation held in spring this year. It may seem that the government has used the parliamentary bill to push its ideas through without having to take the voice of society into account on such an important subject as pure energy. Worse still, such action deprives citizens of the opportunity to benefit from the green solutions promoted by the European Union at a time of rising electricity prices. Prime Minister! Please answer the following questions: 1. What is caused by the acceleration of work on the revision of the RES Act? 2. Is it possible for prosumers to continue to settle in accordance with applicable rules or on a net-metering basis?
